4949 BEB4 D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
4950               	; 			' THE BALL IS ON THE GROUND WHEN HE GETS TO IT.
4951               	; 			'
4952               	; 			' The bounce phase used to be `lx AND 31` -- a byte out of
4953               	; 			' the placement table, which is to say arbitrary. Run onto
4954               	; 			' a screen and the first ball might be at the bottom of its
4955               	; 			' arc, where a jump clears it, or at the top, where it has
4956               	; 			' to be ducked, and nothing on the way in told you which.
4957               	; 			' Sometimes you could keep running and jump it; sometimes
4958               	; 			' the same approach at the same speed could not be jumped
4959               	; 			' at all. That is not difficulty, it is a coin toss.
4960               	; 			'
4961               	; 			' So the phase is worked BACKWARDS from the meeting. He
4962               	; 			' closes 4 px a pass and the ball 2, so they meet in
4963               	; 			' (gap / 6) passes; the phase advances one a pass and wraps
4964               	; 			' at 32, so seeding it with the NEGATIVE of that lands it on
4965               	; 			' phase 0 -- the ground -- exactly as they arrive.
4966               	; 			'
4967               	; 			' The gap is the same either way round: he enters at one
4968               	; 			' wall and the ball starts `stag` in from the other, so it
4969               	; 			' is 232 - stag whichever direction the floor runs.
4970               	; 			'
4971               	; 			' It stays forgiving rather than exact. The arc is flat near
4972               	; 			' the bottom, so anything within eight passes either side is
4973               	; 			' still under the jump; and it only holds for an unbroken
4974               	; 			' run, which is the case the player is entitled to read off
4975               	; 			' the screen. Duck, stop or take a hit and everything after
4976               	; 			' is out of step again -- which is the game.
4977               	; 			obp(li) = lx AND 31
4978 BEB6 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LX,r0
     BEB8 21C2     
4979 BEBA 0201  20 	li r1,7936   ; 31*256
     BEBC 1F00     
4980 BEBE 0541  14 	inv r1
4981 BEC0 5001  18 	szcb r1,r0
4982 BEC2 C040  18 	mov r0,r1
4983 BEC4 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LI,r0
     BEC6 21B4     
4984 BEC8 0980  32 	srl r0,8
4985 BECA 0220  22 	ai r0,array_OBP
     BECC 2264     
4986 BECE D401  30 	movb r1,*r0
4987               	; 			IF lk = OB_BALL THEN
4988 BED0 D020  34 	movb @cvb_LK,r0

     BED6 0200

     C5D8 C676     
5803               cv280
5804               	; 		' HE IS STANDING ON A STEP, so he moves at the STEP's rate: 2 px
5805               	; 		' across and 1 up PER PASS -- the staircase's own slope, and exactly
5806               	; 		' what one phase of esc_tick moves the pattern by.
5807               	; 		'
5808               	; 		' NOT `fdv`, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT. Everything else in this loop
5809               	; 		' is paced by the frame delta, and pacing him that way too made him
5810               	; 		' outrun the steps the moment a pass cost more than one frame: his
5811               	; 		' feet started planted on a tread and ended floating above it.
5812               	; 		' Pacing the ANIMATION by fdv instead is worse -- four phases cannot
5813               	; 		' be stepped by 2 or 3 without aliasing, and the staircase runs
5814               	; 		' backwards (see esc_tick). The only arrangement that cannot drift
5815               	; 		' is the one where both are driven by the same fixed step, so the
5816               	; 		' rider is clocked by the pass, like the steps he is standing on.
5817               	; 		'
5818               	; 		' THE FIRST FEW PASSES ARE THE STEP ON. Walking on, his feet are on
5819               	; 		' the floor and the bottom tread is 4 to 7 px above them, so he
5820               	; 		' climbs at 2 px a pass until he catches it (the tread is rising at
5821               	; 		' 1, so the gap closes at 1 a pass) and then rides with it. Jumping
5822               	; 		' on he lands ON a tread, so eson is 0 and this never runs.
5823               	; 		IF esy < eson THEN esy = esy + 1

6125               	;
6126               	; 	' --- running
6127               	; 	' THE END WALLS ARE MOVEMENT LIMITS, NOT A CLAMP APPLIED AFTERWARDS.
6128               	; 	'
6129               	; 	' They used to be the latter, and that is the whole bug: on screen 7 Kelly
6130               	; 	' walked on to x = 254, the crossing routine then noticed there was no
6131               	; 	' screen 8 and set him to 232 -- a 22 px snap BACKWARDS. He reached the
6132               	; 	' wall and was thrown off it. Testing the limit BEFORE the step means he
6133               	; 	' arrives at the wall and stays there, which is what a wall does.
6134               	; 	' IN THE AIR HE IS BALLISTIC. The horizontal direction is fixed at

6454               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------------------ escalator
6455               	; 	' ONE working escalator per floor, at that floor's alternating end --
6456               	; 	' west, east, west. An escalator drawn where none works would be a step
6457               	; 	' the player runs to and nothing happens.
6458               	; 	' ESCALATORS RUN BOTH WAYS. Stand at the foot and you are carried up;
6459               	; 	' stand at the head of the flight that arrives from the floor below and
6460               	; 	' you are carried down. It is one staircase used from either end, not two
6461               	; 	' -- which is also why the down zone on a floor is the UP zone of the
6462               	; 	' floor beneath it, offset by one band.
6463               	; 	' UP ONLY. Riding down was built at one point and has been taken out
6464               	; 	' again at the reviewer's direction -- these only ever climb.
6465               	; 	'
6466               	; 	' Losing it also removed the re-board lock, which existed solely because
6467               	; 	' stepping off at the head put you inside the zone that rode the same
6468               	; 	' flight back down. With no down ride there is no such zone: a floor's
6469               	; 	' escalator is at the opposite end from the one below it, so arriving at
6470               	; 	' the top of one never lands you on the foot of another.

6506               	; 	' HOW FAR ALONG THE FLIGHT HE IS, measured from its head, in the flight's
6507               	; 	' own x. A step's centre sits at 8 x its index, so the bottom step (9)
6508               	; 	' owns 68..76, the one above it 60..68, and so on up. Both directions
6509               	; 	' land on the same numbers because the east flight is the west one
6510               	; 	' mirrored. The `+ escp` terms are the animation: the whole staircase
6511               	; 	' slides 2 px along and 1 px up per phase, and a rider who ignores that

7029               	;
7030               	; 	' THE VICTROLA, AS CHARACTERS. It never moves, so a sprite spent a
7031               	; 	' scan-line slot on something that can be painted once when the screen is
7032               	; 	' drawn -- and at one 16x16 sprite it could only ever be one colour and
7033               	; 	' one size. Four cells is twice the area for no sprite at all.
7034               	; 	'
7035               	; 	' It stands ON the slab, so it fills the band's rows 2 and 3; row 4 is
7036               	; 	' the floor bar itself and draw_prizes uses row 3 for a collectible,
7037               	; 	' which is why the two never share a screen (genstore keeps prizes off
7038               	; 	' the screens that carry hazards).
7039               	; 	'
7040               	; 	' rdall = 1 paints all four cells, 0 repaints only the top-right one --
7041               	; 	' the pulse. Same address arithmetic either way, so the two cannot drift.
7042               	; 	' A BEAM TOP HANGS OUT OF THE FLOOR ABOVE, and the beam it belongs to has
7043               	; 	' just been taken away. `beam_tops` stamps SLABP -- the floor bar with the
7044               	; 	' support carried up through its lower three pixel rows -- into the slab
7045               	; 	' row above every pillar, and it runs BEFORE the radios and prizes are
7046               	; 	' placed. Clearing a pillar's own cells therefore left three grey pixels
7047               	; 	' dangling from the ceiling with nothing under them.
7048               	; 	'
7049               	; 	' TESTED, NOT ASSUMED: only SLABP is put back to SLAB. The row above the
7050               	; 	' top shopping floor is the ROOF DECK, a different character altogether,
7051               	; 	' and writing a floor bar over it would punch a hole in the roof.
7052               	; 	' A SHELF RUN IS FIVE CELLS WIDE and a radio covers two, so one standing
7053               	; 	' on a counter punched a hole through its middle and left the ends either
7054               	; 	' side -- the same incoherence the dangling pillar had, and worse, because
7055               	; 	' a counter reads as one object. The whole unit goes: walk outward along
7056               	; 	' the band's row 2 while the cell is still a shelf top, clearing it and
7057               	; 	' its bottom half together.
7058               	; 	'
7059               	; 	' Bounded at eight steps. The runs are five, so eight is slack rather
7060               	; 	' than a guess -- and an unbounded walk would run off the end of the row
7061               	; 	' if the name table ever held something unexpected.
7062               	; wipe_shelf:
7063               cvb_WIPE_SHELF
7064               	; 	wsn = 8
7065 CEA8 0200  20 	li r0,2048   ; 8*256
     CEAA 0800

7544               	; 				' THEY WRAP. They do not turn round at the wall.
7545               	; 				' A bouncing obstacle is a pendulum: it has a near
7546               	; 				' end and a far end, and the player learns to stand
7547               	; 				' at the far one and wait. Wrapping makes the floor
7548               	; 				' a STREAM that keeps arriving from the same side,
7549               	; 				' so standing still is never the answer and the
7550               	; 				' player has to keep moving -- which is the whole

8107               	;
8108               	; 	' THE QUARTER-PIXEL ACCUMULATOR. hx is one unsigned byte per screen (2),
8109               	; 	' so there is nowhere to keep a fraction -- the fraction lives here and
8110               	; 	' is spent as whole pixels. hsp4 = 6 walks 1,2,1,2 (1.5 px/frame); 7
8111               	; 	' walks 1,2,2,2 over four frames (1.75); 8 is a flat 2. Two IFs, not a
8112               	; 	' loop and not a divide: hacc is under 4 on entry and hsp4 is at most 8,
8113               	; 	' so it can never need a third. `%` and `/` both compile to a real
8114               	; 	' TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and this runs every frame.
8115               	; 	'
8116               	; 	' 7, NOT 6, AND THE REASON IS MEASURED. Tracked off the reference video
8117               	; 	' (DESIGN.md 4a), the 2600's Kelly covers 0.40 screen widths a second and
8118               	; 	' its Harry 0.193 -- a ratio of 2.07. At hsp4 = 6 ours was 2.67, and the
8119               	; 	' crook visibly strolled. 7 brings it to 2.29. 8 would match the original
8120               	; 	' exactly and cannot be used: Kelly's route here is 1.95x Harry's, so a
8121               	; 	' speed ratio of 2.0 leaves the on-foot chase 0.2 s of slack, which is no
8122               	; 	' chase at all. checkchase.py holds the line.
8123               	; 	' STILL RUNNING FROM THE STAIRS -- and this has to come BEFORE the move,
8124               	; 	' not after it. It sat below the two `IF hmv` blocks, where all it could
8125               	; 	' still reach was the animation counter: he faced the right way, played
8126               	; 	' the run cycle and did not travel a pixel. He stood at the foot of the
8127               	; 	' flight looking busy.
8128               	; 	'
8129               	; 	' TWO THINGS END THE RUN, AND BOTH ARE VISIBLE ON SCREEN.
8130               	; 	'
8131               	; 	' THE KOP ARRIVING ON THIS FLOOR OUTRANKS IT. This used to override the
8132               	; 	' flee as well as the climb, so for the whole of the run he held one
8133               	; 	' heading no matter where Kelly was -- and ran straight into him without
8134               	; 	' appearing to notice he was there. The flee above has already worked out
8135               	; 	' which way is away; clearing the run simply lets it stand.
8136               	; 	'
8137               	; 	' AND REACHING THE END WALL ENDS IT. It used to be a count of passes,
8138               	; 	' which expired somewhere in the middle of a floor and turned him round
8139               	; 	' for no reason the player could see. Running until the wall means every
8140               	; 	' change of direction has something on screen behind it.
8141               	; 	IF hrun > 0 THEN
8142 D748 D020  34 	movb @cvb_HRUN,r0
     D74A 21A0

     D74E 0100

8671 DB80 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
8672 DB82 0450  20 	b *r0
8673               	;
8674               	; 	' ======================================================================
8675               	; 	' COLLISIONS -- pure arithmetic against RAM, no VDP reads
8676               	; 	' ======================================================================
8677               	; 	' Kelly's box is [kfh, kfh + kh) above the slab; an obstacle's is
8678               	; 	' [ohb, oht). The ball's inset hitbox is what makes 8..10 avoidable both
8679               	; 	' ways -- without it there is a height at which the ball can be neither
8680               	; 	' jumped nor ducked, and an unavoidable hazard is not difficulty.
8681               	; 	' ONE HIT PER CONTACT, WITH A REFRACTORY PERIOD.
8682               	; 	'
8683               	; 	' The overlap test is true on every frame the player is touching
8684               	; 	' something, so with no latch at all one clumsy cart charges nine seconds
8685               	; 	' several times over -- and what the player sees is the clock jumping by
8686               	; 	' 27, from which the only available conclusion is that the penalty is
8687               	; 	' broken.
8688               	; 	'
8689               	; 	' A latch that clears on the first non-overlapping frame is still not
8690               	; 	' enough, and the case that breaks it is the BOUNCING BALL: it rises off
8691               	; 	' the player between bounces, which is a genuine loss of contact, so a
8692               	; 	' player standing still under one is taxed on every single bounce while
8693               	; 	' being given no opportunity to do anything about it. Technically two
8694               	; 	' collisions; in play, one situation.
8695               	; 	'
8696               	; 	' So obht() is a countdown, not a flag. A hit sets it to HITREF; it ticks
8697               	; 	' down only while the player is CLEAR, and the same obstacle cannot charge
8698               	; 	' again until it reaches zero. Contact holds the count up, so parking
8699               	; 	' inside something never earns a second penalty either.
8700               	; coll_obst:
8701               cvb_COLL_OBST
8702               	; 	IF klst = ST_ESC THEN RETURN		' riding is invincible, per the
8703 DB84 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLST,r0
     DB86 218C

     DB8A 0300     
8705 DB8C 9001  18 	cb r1,r0
8706 DB8E 1302  14 	jeq cv640
8707 DB90 0460  28 	b @cv639
     DB92 DB98     
8708               cv640

9249               	; 	' NO +8 ON EITHER SIDE, AND THAT IS THE FIX RATHER THAN A TIDY-UP. Both
9250               	; 	' centres are the left edge plus eight, so the eight cancels in the
9251               	; 	' difference -- but adding it first OVERFLOWED THE BYTE. Harry walks to
9252               	; 	' x 253 before he crosses a seam, and 253 + 8 wraps to 5, so a Kop
9253               	; 	' standing at the far LEFT of the screen measured five pixels to a crook
9254               	; 	' walking off the far RIGHT and arrested him across the whole store.
9255               	; 	'
9256               	; 	' Nothing warned: both are plain 8-bit variables, the wrap is silent, and
9257               	; 	' the catch it produces looks like a generous hitbox rather than like
9258               	; 	' arithmetic. The obstacle test has the same shape but cannot reach it --
9259               	; 	' obx tops out at 240 and klx at XWALL, so neither sum passes 255.

9322               	; 	' KELLY IS SPRITES 0, 1 AND 2 and nothing else ever is -- the VDP drops
9323               	; 	' the highest-numbered sprites on an over-full scanline, so the lowest
9324               	; 	' slots are the ones that can never disappear, and the player is the one
9325               	; 	' thing that must never disappear.
9326               	; 	'
9327               	; 	' THE BAND SPRITES SHARE A y ON PURPOSE. Slots 0 and 1 both sit at ky and
9328               	; 	' between them cover rows 0-15; slot 2 sits at ky+16. Boxes 0/1 and box 2
9329               	; 	' never share a scanline, so an actor costs at most TWO boxes on any line
9330               	; 	' -- and the VDP counts boxes, not pixels, so an empty overlap would have
9331               	; 	' cost just as much as a full one. Two actors meeting is four, exactly
9332               	; 	' the per-line limit, which is why obstacles are suppressed on Harry's
9333               	; 	' floor rather than merely being a kindness.

9456               cv706
9457               	; 		' FOUR RUN FRAMES FROM TWO BITS of the animation counter, and no
9458               	; 		' divide -- `/` compiles to a real TMS9900 DIV (CLAUDE.md 3A) and
9459               	; 		' this is per-frame code. Two adjacent bits give 0,4,8,12.
9460               	; 		'
9461               	; 		' KELLY USES BITS 2 AND 3 AND HARRY USES 3 AND 4, because a stride
9462               	; 		' has to cover about a stride's worth of ground or the figure
9463               	; 		' skates. Kelly runs 4 px a frame, so four frames a pose is 16 px;
9464               	; 		' Harry runs 1.75, so eight frames a pose is 14. Matching the two
9465               	; 		' rates to the two speeds is what keeps both looking like running.
9466               	; 		kq = P_KLEG1
9467 E1CC 0200  20 	li r0,14336   ; 56*256
     E1CE 3800

10184 E810 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10185 E812 0450  20 	b *r0
10186               	;
10187               	; 	' ======================================================================
10188               	; 	' THE SCANNER
10189               	; 	' ======================================================================
10190               	; 	' Rows 21-23, 16 chars wide, centred: 48 characters whose PATTERNS are
10191               	; 	' poked directly, so a moving dot costs two writes and no name-table
10192               	; 	' traffic. With the flip (DESIGN.md 2a) this is how you know where Harry
10193               	; 	' is at all -- he can be one pixel off-screen and completely invisible.
10194               	; 	'
10195               	; 	' Rows 21-23 all sit in the THIRD screen third, so every scanner pattern
10196               	; 	' is at base 4096 and no third-selection arithmetic is needed.
10197               	; 	'
10198               	; 	' BOTH DOTS ARE WHITE and Kelly's BLINKS. The manual wants Kelly black
10199               	; 	' and Harry white, but this is the TMS9918's two-colours-per-8x1-cell
10200               	; 	' mode: a black dot and a white dot on the same pixel row of the same
10201               	; 	' character cannot both exist. Blinking distinguishes them with one
10202               	; 	' colour, which is what RallyX does with its player dot.
10203               	; 	' THE STEPS MOVE, AND EVERY ESCALATOR ON SCREEN MOVES WITH THEM. A flight
10204               	; 	' is twelve characters -- six per direction, sliced out of a rendered
10205               	; 	' 64x40 bitmap by genart.py -- so rewriting those twelve PATTERNS animates
10206               	; 	' the lot: 96 bytes, no name-table traffic, and the same cost whether one
10207               	; 	' flight is visible or ten. The phases slide the cells ALONG the slope, so
10208               	; 	' the steps travel up the flight rather than drifting sideways.
10209               	; esc_tick:
10210               cvb_ESC_TICK
10211               	; 	' ONLY SCREENS 0 AND 7 CARRY A FLIGHT, so six screens in eight skip this
10212               	; 	' entirely -- which is what makes rewriting the cells every frame
10213               	; 	' affordable. Nested, not `klsc > 0 AND klsc < 7`: the 9900 backend
10214               	; 	' miscompiles a compare-AND-compare (CLAUDE.md 3A).
10215               	; 	IF klsc > 0 THEN
10216 E814 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E816 217A     
10217 E818 0201  20 	li r1,256   ; 1*256
     E81A 0100     
10218 E81C 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10219 E81E 1402  14 	jhe cv774
10220 E820 0460  28 	b @cv773
     E822 E838     
10221               cv774
10222               	; 		IF klsc < 7 THEN RETURN
10223 E824 D020  34 	movb @cvb_KLSC,r0
     E826 217A     
10224 E828 0201  20 	li r1,1792   ; 7*256
     E82A 0700     
10225 E82C 9040  18 	cb r0,r1
10226 E82E 1A02  14 	jl cv776
10227 E830 0460  28 	b @cv775
     E832 E838     
10228               cv776
10229 E834 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10230 E836 0450  20 	b *r0
10231               cv775
10232               	; 	END IF
10233               cv773
10234               	; 	' ONE PHASE PER PASS, AND NEVER MORE. There are four phases covering an
10235               	; 	' 8 px period, so advancing by the frame delta ALIASES: at fdv 2 the
10236               	; 	' pattern flips between two positions half a period apart and the
10237               	; 	' direction stops being readable, and at fdv 3 the sequence runs 0,3,2,1
10238               	; 	' -- the staircase visibly runs BACKWARDS, carrying its steps down while
10239               	; 	' the rider goes up. That is the wagon-wheel effect, and no amount of
10240               	; 	' phase arithmetic fixes it: a 4-phase cycle can only be stepped by 1.
10241               	; 	'
10242               	; 	' So the ANIMATION is the clock here, and the rider is paced from it --
10243               	; 	' see move_kelly, which advances him by the same fixed 2-and-1 per pass
10244               	; 	' rather than by fdv. Locking the two together is the only way they
10245               	; 	' cannot drift, and it is what "the player moves at the rate the steps
10246               	; 	' carry him" actually means. The cost is that a ride takes 36 passes
10247               	; 	' rather than 36 frames, so it slows down when the loop does -- which is
10248               	; 	' the honest behaviour for a machine that is carrying you.
10249               	; 	escp = escp + 1
10250 E838 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ESCP,r0
     E83A 20B0

     E83E 0100

     E842 20B0

     E846 0400

10262               	; 	' ONLY THE CELLS THAT MOVE, AND ONLY THIS SCREEN'S. genart.py measures
10263               	; 	' which cells move -- the step cells and the COMPOSITE copies of them
10264               	; 	' that cross a floor -- and groups them WEST then EAST, because only one
10265               	; 	' direction is ever on screen: screen 0 carries a west flight and screen
10266               	; 	' 7 an east one.
10267               	; 	'
10268               	; 	' THIS IS THE ONLY PER-PASS WORK UNIQUE TO THESE TWO SCREENS, and it is
10269               	; 	' `define_char`, which in bitmap mode is LDIRVM3 -- the TRIPLE copy, once
10270               	; 	' per screen third. It therefore costs three times what the character
10271               	; 	' count suggests, which is why these screens run slower than the rest.
10272               	; 	'
10273               	; 	' THE WEST SCREEN USED TO COST HALF AS MUCH AGAIN, because it carries TWO
10274               	; 	' flights: floor 1's crosses into a shop floor and floor 3's into the
10275               	; 	' roof, so it needed both composite sets -- nine characters against the
10276               	; 	' east's six, 216 bytes a pass against 144. It measured 20 passes a
10277               	; 	' second where the east screen managed 24-26. The two sets are
10278               	; 	' PIXEL-IDENTICAL and are never wanted in the same screen THIRD, so they
10279               	; 	' share their characters now and are told apart by the per-third colour
10280               	; 	' table (see esc_deck_col in setup). Six either side.

10543 EA92 C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10544 EA94 0450  20 	b *r0
10545               	;
10546               	; 	' ------------------------------------------------- the scanner's furniture
10547               	; 	' Without this the scanner is two dots in a void: you can see THAT Harry is
10548               	; 	' somewhere, but not what he is near, which is the one thing you actually
10549               	; 	' need when he is off-screen. Floor lines say which level, the slashes say
10550               	; 	' where that floor's escalator is, and the bar says where the elevator is --
10551               	; 	' exactly the three things the manual lists.
10552               	; 	'
10553               	; 	' FURNITURE AND DOTS ARE SEGREGATED BY PIXEL ROW ON PURPOSE, and a level
10554               	; 	' band is now FOUR rows, one per colour, because a pixel row carries
10555               	; 	' exactly one colour here:
10556               	; 	'
10557               	; 	'   +0  GREY    escalator head, and the elevator car
10558               	; 	'   +1  GREY    the air of that floor
10559               	; 	'   +2  GREY
10560               	; 	'   +3  YELLOW  the floor line -- and NOTHING else goes here
10561               	; 	'
10562               	; 	' THE FIRST THREE ROWS ARE THE FLOOR'S AIR AND EVERYTHING LIVES IN THEM:
10563               	; 	' the escalator's three-step diagonal, the lift car, and both actors, all
10564               	; 	' three pixels tall. They are grey in the table, and the Kop and the
10565               	; 	' crook recolour their own CHARACTER as they move (scan_mark) -- which is
10566               	; 	' what lets both of them fill the band instead of getting a pixel row
10567               	; 	' each. Eight pixels of this canvas is half a screen of the store, so
10568               	; 	' they are almost never in one character; when they are, they merge.
10569               	; 	'
10570               	; 	' Shrinking from six rows bought eight empty pixel rows, four above the
10571               	; 	' instrument and four below, so it no longer butts against the shop floor
10572               	; 	' above it or the bottom of the screen.
10573               	; 	'
10574               	; 	' The dots are erased by ANDing their bits out, so anything sharing a row
10575               	; 	' with one is rubbed away wherever an actor has passed -- which is why
10576               	; 	' scan_escs and scan_elev redraw every tick rather than once.
10577               	; scan_furn:
10578               cvb_SCAN_FURN

10850               	;
10851               	; 	' THE FLIGHT IS BLACK, and it has to be written per character because the
10852               	; 	' colour table only knows rows -- the same reason the two actors carry
10853               	; 	' their own colour (scan_mark).
10854               	; 	'
10855               	; 	' This runs BEFORE the markers every tick, which is the whole ordering
10856               	; 	' the radar needs: the lift car is painted first and the flights next, so
10857               	; 	' a Kop or a crook standing on either of them paints over it. Nothing
10858               	; 	' moves aside to make room -- the pixels are simply ORed together and the
10859               	; 	' last colour written wins, so an actor in the lift shaft shows as an
10860               	; 	' actor rather than shifting anywhere.
10861               	; scan_escc:
10862               cvb_SCAN_ESCC
10863               	; 	#sdx = #sda + 8192

     ECC8 20A2

     ECCC 2000

     ECD0 20AC     
10867               	; 	VPOKE #sdx,SC_KOP
10868 ECD2 0200  20 	li r0,7168   ; 28*256
     ECD4 1C00

     ECEC 0002

10878 ECEE C03A  30 	mov *r10+,r0
10879 ECF0 0450  20 	b *r0
10880               	;
10881               	; 	' fbase = the top pixel row of level fl's FOUR px band, without a
10882               	; 	' multiply. Four levels at four rows is 16 of the canvas's 24 pixel

10996               	;
10997               	; 	' Blank the canvas by WRITING zeros rather than copying 384 of them out
10998               	; 	' of ROM. This runs twice a game -- at setup and on a new Krook -- so a
10999               	; 	' loop costs nothing anybody can perceive, and it hands the fixed area
11000               	; 	' back the best part of half a kilobyte, which is the budget that
11001               	; 	' actually binds. Paced in bursts: a few hundred VDP writes in a single
11002               	; 	' frame are silently dropped (CLAUDE.md 3A).

11076               	;
11077               	; 	' THE TWO ACTORS ARE SPRITES; EVERYTHING ELSE IS CHARACTERS.
11078               	; 	'
11079               	; 	' That split is what makes the instrument simple. The floor lines, the
11080               	; 	' flights and the lift car do not move relative to the canvas, so they
11081               	; 	' are pixels in the scanner's own characters and cost nothing to keep.
11082               	; 	' The Kop and the crook DO move, and as sprites they need no colour
11083               	; 	' table, no erase and no priority arithmetic -- they draw over the
11084               	; 	' furniture because sprites always do, and nothing shifts to make room.
11085               	; 	'
11086               	; 	' IT ALSO REMOVED THE BLINK. Kelly flashed because a pixel row of this
11087               	; 	' canvas can only carry one colour, so at close range the two markers
11088               	; 	' merged and the blink was the only thing left to tell them apart. Two
11089               	; 	' sprites are two colours wherever they stand.
11090               	; 	'
11091               	; 	' The furniture is drawn ONCE, by scan_furn. It used to be redrawn every
11092               	; 	' tick because erasing a character-based marker ANDed bits out of
11093               	; 	' whatever it was standing on; there is nothing left to rub it away. The
11094               	; 	' car still redraws itself, because the car actually moves.
11095               	; 	' -- the lift car. IT WAS FLICKERING, and the cause was not the drawing
11096               	; 	' but the ERASING: scan_elev cleared its three cells and put them back
11097               	; 	' every tick, whether or not the car had moved, so several times a second
11098               	; 	' there was a window with the pixels off. A sprite has no erase at all --
11099               	; 	' it is placed, and the VDP does the rest -- and slot 26 sits BELOW the
11100               	; 	' two markers, so the Kop and the crook pass over it without either of
11101               	; 	' them having to know the car exists.
11102               	; 	say = 3 - elvl
11103 EE80 D020  34 	movb @cvb_ELVL,r0
     EE82 2131

11797               	;
11798               	; 	' THE BONUS IS COUNTED OUT, NOT AWARDED. Dropping the whole sum into the
11799               	; 	' score in one frame tells the player a number; ticking the clock down a
11800               	; 	' unit at a time, with the score climbing and a beat under each step,
11801               	; 	' tells them what the number was FOR. It is the pay-off for every second
11802               	; 	' they saved, and every arcade game of this era spends a moment on it.
11803               	; 	'
11804               	; 	' It also costs nothing to get right: the loop already has to run down
11805               	; 	' `tsec`, so the animation IS the calculation, and the repeated-addition
11806               	; 	' note below stops applying -- each step adds one band's worth.
11807               	; 	'
11808               	; 	' x100 for Krooks 1-9, x200 for 10-15, x300 from 16. Two independent
11809               	; 	' sources agree on those bands; the manual's own wording suggests
11810               	; 	' 1-8 / 9-16 / 17+ and DESIGN.md 0 records the disagreement.
11811               	; 	'
11812               	; 	' #bval MUST BE 16-BIT: 300 does not fit in a byte, and a plain variable
11813               	; 	' would silently truncate it to 44 (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11814               	; bonus_count:
11815               cvb_BONUS_COUNT

     F538 F48A     
11917               	;
11918               	; 	' `calc_bonus` USED TO LIVE HERE and computed the whole sum by repeated
11919               	; 	' addition, to dodge two hazards at once: `tsec * bmul` is an 8-bit
11920               	; 	' product (a 50-unit capture in the x300 band pays 15,000, six times past
11921               	; 	' a byte), and multiplying properly lands on the MPY/r0 hazard where a
11922               	; 	' 16-bit variable read straight after being multiplied returns the
11923               	; 	' product's high word. Counting the bonus out a unit at a time (above)
11924               	; 	' removes the multiply entirely rather than working around it -- the loop
11925               	; 	' the player watches IS the arithmetic.
11926               	; 	'
11927               	; 	' It also fixed the last-tick case for free: `bn_loop` tests `tsec = 0`
11928               	; 	' on entry, where the old `FOR bi = 1 TO tsec` would have run its body
11929               	; 	' once and paid a full multiplier for no time at all (CLAUDE.md 3A).
11930               	;
11931               	; do_escape:
11932               cvb_DO_ESCAPE
11933               	; 	escapd = 0

     F668 AA16     
12065               	;
12066               	; 	' Silence every channel FIRST. SOUND latches, and the per-channel decay
12067               	; 	' counters are not ticked inside this loop -- a tone still sounding when
12068               	; 	' the pause starts would hang for its whole duration, which is the classic
12069               	; 	' CVBasic sticky-audio failure.
12070               	; 	' EVERY CHANNEL OFF, AND THE DECAY COUNTERS WITH THEM. The counters are
12071               	; 	' ticked by the main loop, so anything still sounding when the loop stops
12072               	; 	' -- for a capture, a death, the bonus count -- sustains for as long as
12073               	; 	' whatever replaced the loop takes. That is where the long beep over the
12074               	; 	' bonus tally came from: not the tally's own note, which turns itself off
12075               	; 	' after every unit, but a footstep or a hit that was still ringing when
12076               	; 	' Harry was caught.
12077               	; snd_off:
12078               cvb_SND_OFF

12532               	;
12533               	; 	' The font stays in bank 0: it is small, and keeping one readable thing
12534               	; 	' unbanked makes a bank-selection mistake obvious (text survives, art does
12535               	; 	' not) instead of producing a uniformly blank screen.
12536               	; 	INCLUDE "font.bas"
12537               	; 	' Keystone Kapers font -- GENERATED by assets/genfont.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12538               	; 	'
12539               	; 	' Characters 32-90 (59 of them), 8 bytes each = 472 bytes. Williams' cabinet face:
12540               	; 	' heavy, squared, flat terminals, no serifs -- drawn 5x7 inside the 8x8 cell so a
12541               	; 	' blank column at the right and a blank row at the bottom keep it legible.
12542               	; 	'
12543               	; 	' Loaded with DEFINE CHAR 32,59,font_bits. The run must be CONTIGUOUS, so slots
12544               	; 	' with no glyph emit blanks rather than being skipped -- a gap would shift every
12545               	; 	' letter after it.
12546               	;
12547               	; font_bits:
12548               cvb_FONT_BITS
12549               	; 	DATA BYTE $00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00,$00	' space

12667               	;
12668               	; 	' EVERYTHING BELOW THIS LINE IS ASSEMBLED INTO BANK 1, so the INCLUDE order
12669               	; 	' is load-bearing and nothing but data may follow it. Putting the directive
12670               	; 	' above font.bas would sweep the font in too.
12671               	; 	#if TI994A
12672               	; 	BANK 1
12673      03E6     BANK_0_FREE:	EQU >fffe-$

12680               	; 	INCLUDE "art.bas"
12681               	; 	' Keystone Kapers art -- GENERATED by assets/genart.py. Do not edit; regenerate.
12682               	; 	'
12683               	; 	' EVERY SPRITE IS 16x16: sprite size and magnification are GLOBAL on the
12684               	; 	' TMS9918, so small objects are drawn small INSIDE a 16x16 box rather than
12685               	; 	' being small sprites.
12686               	; 	'
12687               	; 	' EVERY 8-PX OBJECT SITS IN ROWS 8-15 OF ITS BOX, ducked Kelly included, so
12688               	; 	' one rule places them all: bottom edge = y + 16, and a thing resting on the
12689               	; 	' floor is at y = FLOORY - 16.
12690               	; 	'
12691               	; 	' Sprite bytes are QUADRANT-ORDERED: 16 rows of the left half, then 16 of
12692               	; 	' the right. Every block is an EVEN number of bytes -- an odd DATA BYTE run
12693               	; 	' silently misaligns every word table after it (CLAUDE.md 3A).
12694               	;
12695               	; spr_kelly:	' Kelly: RIGHT hat/face/tunic/tunic-arm-up/duck-hat/duck-face, then the same LEFT (+24), then the FOUR shared run frames. Patterns 0..60
12696               cvb_SPR_KELLY

13456               	;
13457               	; 	' -------------------------------------------------- scanner canvas
13458               	; 	' Chars 208-255: 16 cols x 3 rows over screen rows 21-23. Blank to start;
13459               	; 	' dots are plotted by VPOKEing the PATTERN table (rows 21-23 sit in the
13460               	; 	' third screen third, so every scanner pattern is at base 4096).
13461               	;
13462               	; 	' --------------------------------------------------- font colours
13463               	; 	' Chars 32-90, EIGHT bytes each. Without this the font keeps whatever
13464               	; 	' CVBasic left in the colour table, which on a green store made the HUD
13465               	; 	' unreadable.
13466               	;
13467               	; scan_col3:	' one 8-byte block per canvas row: 4 px of GREY margin, then a 3 px band of furniture GREY and a yellow floor line, per 4 px level, on dark green. The Kop and the crook are coloured PER CHARACTER at run time -- see scan_set
13468               cvb_SCAN_COL3
13469               	; 	DATA BYTE $EE,$EE,$EE,$EE,$EC,$EC,$EC,$BC
13470 6B2A EEEE     	byte >ee,>ee,>ee,>ee,>ec,>ec,>ec,>bc
     6B2C EEEE     
     6B2E ECEC     
     6B30 ECBC

     6B3E EEEE     
     6B40 EEEE

13564               	; 	INCLUDE "store.bas"
13565               	; 	' Keystone Kapers store -- GENERATED by assets/genstore.py. Do not edit.
13566               	; 	'
13567               	; 	' EIGHT band templates of 5 rows x 32 cols (160 bytes each) plus a 32-byte
13568               	; 	' index over (level, screen). The whole 8-screen, 4-level building is about
13569               	; 	' 1.3 KB instead of the 5,120 bytes it would cost to store literally.
13570               	; 	'
13571               	; 	' All eight templates are ONE contiguous block under ONE label, so a band
13572               	; 	' blit is
13573               	; 	'     SCREEN stor_tpl, tpl*160, toprow*32, 32, 5, 32
13574               	; 	' with no label ladder -- SCREEN needs a literal label and a label cannot be
13575               	; 	' chosen by a variable, which is why RallyX needed a 4-way IF over its maze
13576               	; 	' tables. One block sidesteps it.
13577               	; 	'
13578               	; 	' 160 is EVEN, so this cannot leave the location counter odd and misalign
13579               	; 	' the word tables defined after it.
13580               	; 	'
13581               	; 	' template ids:
13582               	; 	'   0 T_AISLE_A
13583               	; 	'   1 T_AISLE_B
13584               	; 	'   2 T_ESC_W
13585               	; 	'   3 T_ESC_E
13586               	; 	'   4 T_ELEV
13587               	; 	'   5 T_ROOF
13588               	; 	'   6 T_ROOF_W
13589               	; 	'   7 T_ROOF_E
13590               	; 	'   8 T_END_W
13591               	; 	'   9 T_END_E
13592               	;
13593               	; stor_tpl:	' 10 templates x 160 bytes
13594               cvb_STOR_TPL
13595               	; 	DATA BYTE 149,149,149,149,105,149,149,149,149,149,149,105,149,149,149,149
